   - Path A → F → C → G → R → O → Q → I → N has a length of 7.

1. **Direct Path:**
   - \( A \to E \to B \to T \to Q \to I \)

2. **Alternative Paths:**
   - \( A \to E \to B \to N \to Q \to I \)
   - \( A \to F \to S \to T \to Q \to I \)
   - \( A \to F \to S \to C \to G \to R \to O \to Q \to I \)

### Cut Set Identification:
To disconnect A and I, we need to remove nodes that lie on all possible paths between them.

1. **Node \( Q \):**
   - Removing \( Q \) will break the paths \( A \to E \to B \to T \to Q \to I \), \( A \to E \to B \to N \to Q \to I \), and \( A \to F \to S \to T \to Q \to I \). However, it does not cover paths through \( O \).

2. **Node \( T \):**
   - Removing \( T \) will break paths \( A \to E \to B \to T \to Q \to I \) and \( A \to F \to S \to T \to Q \to I \). It also does not cover paths through \( O \).

Considering the need to cover all paths, we see that removing a single node is not sufficient. The minimal cut set must include multiple nodes to cover all possible connections:

### Removing Nodes:
1. **Node \( Q \) and \( T \):**
   - Removing both \( Q \) and \( T \) would break all direct paths. However, we need to ensure there are no remaining alternative paths.

2. **Additional Node \( B \):**
   - Removing \( B \) will ensure that the paths through \( E \to B \to T \) and \( E \to B \to N \) are also broken.

Thus, the minimal set of nodes to remove to ensure complete disconnection of \( A \) and \( I \) is:
